<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove a uniform index theorem for connected projective semi-log-canonical log Calabi–Yau pairs in every fixed dimension at least four over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. For boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set, a single multiple of the log canonical divisor is Cartier and linearly trivial. The multiple depends only on the dimension and coefficient set, not on the number of irreducible components. Together with the established theorem in dimensions at most three, this resolves the finite-rational-coefficient semi-log-canonical index conjecture.

- **claim-uniform-slc-index** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $d\ge4$ and every finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, there is an integer $a(d,\Phi)>0$ with the following property. Let $(X,B)$ be a connected projective equidimensional semi-log-canonical log Calabi–Yau pair of dimension $d$ over $k$, with every nonzero coefficient of $B$ in $\Phi$. Then $$a(d,\Phi)(K_X+B)\text{ is Cartier}, \qquad \mathcal O_X\bigl(a(d,\Phi)(K_X+B)\bigr)\simeq\mathcal O_X.$$
  - Explanation: A common multiple makes the log canonical divisor Cartier and trivial for the stated semi-log-canonical pairs. Its dependence is confined to the dimension and the finite rational coefficient set.
- **claim-crepant-form-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-form-crepant)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ and $(Y,\Delta_Y)$ be normal projective pairs, and let $\theta_X,\theta_Y$ be degree-$m$ rational forms satisfying <a href="#eq:form-trivialization" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:form-trivialization">[eq:form-trivialization]</a> for the respective boundaries. A birational map $f:X\dashrightarrow Y$ is $B$-birational if and only if $$f^*\theta_Y=c\theta_X \quad\text{for some }c\in\mathbb C^*.$$
  - Explanation: For the prescribed log trivializing forms, crepant birationality is equivalent to proportionality under pullback. The scalar comes from a rational function with zero divisor on a normal projective integral common resolution.
- **claim-normal-index-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-input-normal-index)): For every integer $d\ge0$ and every rational set $I\subset[0,1]$ satisfying the descending chain condition, there is an integer $a_{\mathrm{lc}}(d,I)>0$ such that every normal projective integral lc pair $(X,\Delta)$ of dimension $d$, with coefficients in $I$ and $K_X+\Delta\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, satisfies $$a_{\mathrm{lc}}(d,I)(K_X+\Delta)\sim0.$$
  - Explanation: The companion normal index theorem supplies a common principal multiple for normal log canonical pairs with rational DCC coefficients and rationally trivial log canonical divisor.
- **claim-good-minimal-model-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-input-good-model)): A projective lc pair over $\mathbb C$ with effective rational boundary and pseudo-effective log canonical divisor has a good log minimal model. In particular its adjoint is nonvanishing and its transform on the good model is semiample.
  - Explanation: The companion good-model theorem supplies nonvanishing and a semiample transform for the effective rational log canonical pair with pseudo-effective adjoint.
- **claim-bounded-comparison-base** (bound, [source](#stmt-input-bounded-base)): Fix $n$. Let $h:V\to Y$ be a contraction over $\mathbb C$, where $V$ is klt of dimension $n$, $K_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$, and $0<b=\dim Y<n$. Suppose that a smooth projective resolution of $Y$ is rationally connected. Then $Y$ is isomorphic in codimension one to a normal projective variety $\widehat Y$ in a bounded family depending only on $n$. In particular $\widehat Y$ has a very ample Cartier divisor $H$ for which $$\label{eq:bounded-base-degrees} H^b\le C_n,\qquad \lvert K_{\widehat Y}\cdot H^{b-1}\rvert\le C_n.$$ The canonical intersection is the degree of the canonical Weil divisor on a general complete-intersection curve in the smooth locus.
  - Explanation: A rationally connected base of the specified contraction admits a bounded comparison model up to isomorphism in codimension one. Bounded embeddings control its polarization degree and canonical intersection.
- **claim-top-form-hodge-invariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hodge-basic)): The Hodge structure $T(U)$ is birationally invariant. Cup product with any rational divisor class annihilates $T(U)$; in particular, $T(U)$ is primitive for every rational ample class. Moreover, if $f:Z\to U$ is a morphism from a smooth projective variety with $\dim f(Z)<n$, then $f^*T(U)=0$.
  - Explanation: The Hodge substructure generated by top forms survives birational modification, is annihilated by rational divisor classes, and pulls back trivially through maps with lower-dimensional image.
- **claim-quantitative-hodge-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-hodge-gap)): Let $M$ be a compact Kähler manifold of complex dimension $2n$, where $n\ge1$, let $D$ be a Kähler form, and let $H$ be a smooth closed semipositive $(1,1)$-form of pointwise rank at most $n$. If $z\in H^{n,n}(M)$ satisfies $[H]^n z=0$, then, for every $R\ge1$, $$\label{hodge:gap} \lVert z\rVert_{D+RH} \le R^{(n-\delta_n)/2}\lVert z\rVert_{D+H}, \qquad \delta_n=\frac{1}{\binom{2n}{n}+2}.$$
  - Explanation: The vanishing cup product forces a strict loss in the maximal growth exponent of the middle-degree Hodge norm under the specified semipositive metric stretching.
- **claim-diagonal-hodge-rank-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-diagonal)): Fix an integer $n\ge2$ and a real number $c>0$. Let $X$ be a normal projective complex $n$-fold with canonical singularities and $K_X\sim0$ as an integral principal divisor. Suppose $h$ is an ample rational divisor such that $$1\le h^n\le2, \qquad \epsilon(h;x)>c \quad\text{at very general smooth points }x\in X.$$ Then $r(U)=\dim_\mathbb QT(U)$, for any smooth projective resolution $U$ of $X$, is bounded by a constant depending only on $n$ and $c$.
  - Explanation: Normalized polarization volume and a uniform positive Seshadri lower bound control the top-form Hodge rank. The diagonal construction makes uncontrolled corrections invisible to that Hodge substructure.
- **claim-uniform-top-form-rank** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-rank)): For every integer $n\ge2$ there is a constant $R_n$ with the following property. Let $T$ be a projective $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal complex $n$-fold with $K_T\sim0$. Suppose that a smooth projective resolution $U$ has $h^1(U,\mathcal O_U)=0$, and that every positive-dimensional rational image of $T$ of dimension less than $n$ has a rationally connected smooth projective resolution. Then $$r(T)=\dim_{\mathbb Q}T(U)\le R_n.$$
  - Explanation: The top-form Hodge rank is bounded solely by dimension under terminality, principal canonical class, vanishing irregularity, and the stated condition on smaller rational images.
- **claim-generic-chain-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-input-rank-chains)): A finite nonempty collection of marked covering families determines a rational fibration $\xi:V\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. If its generic fibre, called a leaf, has dimension $h$, then $1\le h\le\dim V$, every generic member lies in a leaf, and at most $h$ generic movements reach a point generic in the leaf over the algebraic closure of the field of its starting mark. Suppose that, for each family, either $$\gamma(P\|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\le B, \qquad\text{or}\qquad \dim C_b=1,\quad \frac{P\cdot C_b}{\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_{u(b)}C_b}\le B.$$ For a smooth model $F$ of the geometric generic leaf, with $P$ pulled back, one then has $$\label{eq:rank:chain-bound} \gamma(P\|_F;F)\le hB, \qquad (P\|_F)^h\le(hB)^h.$$
  - Explanation: Marked covering families generate geometrically integral leaves. Bounds on member orders or curve ratios propagate to a leaf order bound and the corresponding powered volume bound.
- **claim-tracking-canonical-degree-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-input-rank-tracking)): Let $Z$ be projective, $\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt of dimension $d\ge2$, and let $N$ be big, nef and semiample. If $d+1$ equally spaced positive rational levels, with spacing $\Delta>0$, lie strictly between $(N^d)^{1/d}$ and $\gamma(N;Z)$, there is a covering family of subvarieties $W\subset Z$, of dimension $0<e<d$, such that a smooth geometric generic model $W^*$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} N^eW&\le(2/\Delta)^{d-e}N^d,\label{eq:rank:tracking-degree}\\ K_{W^*}(N\|_{W^*})^{e-1} &\le\bigl(K_Z+(2d/\Delta)N\bigr)\|_W(N\|_W)^{e-1}. \label{eq:rank:tracking-canonical} \end{aligned}$$ If a smooth model of $Z$ has nonnegative Kodaira dimension, so does $W^*$. The same inheritance statement holds for arbitrary covering families.
  - Explanation: Separated section-order levels produce moving subvarieties with controlled polarized and canonical degrees. Nonnegative Kodaira dimension passes to their smooth geometric generic models.
- **claim-small-iitaka-fibre-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-input-rank-small-fibre)): Fix $e\ge1$ and $C'>0$. Let $W_i$ be smooth projective $e$-folds with $\kappa(W_i)\ge0$, and let $L_i$ be big, nef, semiample rational divisors such that $$L_i^e\longrightarrow0, \qquad K_{W_i}L_i^{e-1}\le C'L_i^e.$$ After passing to a subsequence and resolving their Iitaka fibrations, the smooth geometric generic fibres $U_i$ have a fixed positive dimension, satisfy $\kappa(U_i)=0$, and obey $$\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(K_{U_i}+L_i\|_{U_i})\longrightarrow0.$$ The restrictions $L_i\|_{U_i}$ remain big, nef and semiample. When the Iitaka base is a point, $U_i$ is the entire smooth model.
  - Explanation: Vanishing polarization volumes with controlled canonical degrees lead, after subsequence and resolution, to positive-dimensional Iitaka fibres of Kodaira dimension zero with vanishing adjoint volumes.
- **claim-large-rank-low-order-families** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-low-order)): Fix $n\ge2$ and $\eta>0$. There is a number $R(n,\eta)$ such that if $T$ is as in Theorem <a href="#thm:rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rank">9</a> and $r(T)>R(n,\eta)$, the following holds. For every normal projective canonical $V$ birational to $T$ with $K_V\sim0$, and every ample rational Cartier divisor $P$ on $V$, there is a marked covering family of subvarieties $C_b$ with $0<\dim C_b<n$, whose smooth geometric generic models satisfy $$\label{eq:rank:low-order} \kappa(C_b^*)=0, \qquad \gamma(P\|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\le\eta(P^n)^{1/n},$$ where the mark is smooth on the member.
  - Explanation: Hypothetically large Hodge rank forces covering members of Kodaira dimension zero with small complete-system orders for every ample rational polarization on a canonical birational model.
- **claim-chain-field-attainment** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-attainment)): If $0<\eta<1/n$, every chain field obtained from a family in Lemma <a href="#lem:rank:low-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:rank:low-order">13</a> is attained and has transcendence degree strictly between $0$ and $n$.
  - Explanation: The chain field is proper and is realized by a contraction on a small terminal model. The proof identifies its section-ratio field and uses a terminating good-model program.
- **claim-calibrated-big-divisor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-calibration)): Let $h:X\to Y$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, where $X$ is a $\mathbb Q$-factorial canonical $n$-fold with $K_X\sim0$ and irregularity zero on a resolution. Suppose that $0<b:=\dim Y<n$ and that no prime divisor of $X$ maps into a subset of codimension at least two in $Y$. Let $\widehat Y$ be a normal projective variety isomorphic to $Y$ in codimension one, and let $H$ be a very ample divisor on $\widehat Y$ satisfying $$H^b\le N_0, \qquad \bigl\|K_{\widehat Y}\cdot H^{b-1}\bigr\|\le N_0.$$ Pullback from the common smooth open subsets of the bases defines $$W=h^*\mathop{\mathrm{Cl}}(Y)_{\mathbb R} =h^*\mathop{\mathrm{Cl}}(\widehat Y)_{\mathbb R} \subset N^1(X)_{\mathbb R}, \qquad \ell(h^*L)=L\cdot H^{b-1}.$$ Let $A=h^*H$, represented by an effective integral Weil divisor. There is a linear extension $\ell_X:N^1(X)_{\mathbb R}\to\mathbb R$ of $\ell$, nonnegative on the pseudo-effective cone, and there are a rational number $\delta>0$ and a big rational divisor $D$ such that $$\label{eq:rank:calibration} c\delta\le v(D)\le C\delta, \qquad D-\frac{\delta}{3}A\text{ is big}, \qquad \ell_X(D)\le C\delta.$$ Here $v(D)=\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(D)^{1/n}$, $c>0$ depends only on $n$, and $C$ depends only on $n,N_0$.
  - Explanation: A supporting functional extending base degree and a calibrated big divisor put volume, a base-direction bigness margin, and functional degree on a common scale. The existence proof uses separation, continuity, and good models.
- **claim-linear-rank-section-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-section-count)): Let $b\ge1$. A torsion-free slope-semistable sheaf $\mathcal G$ on $\mathbb P^b_{\mathbb C}$, of rank $u$ and slope $\mu$ with respect to $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^b}(1)$, satisfies $$h^0(\mathbb P^b,\mathcal G) \le C_bu\bigl(1+\max\{0,\mu\}\bigr)^b,$$ where $C_b$ depends only on $b$.
  - Explanation: The section bound for a torsion-free semistable sheaf on projective space is linear in its rank. Finite power maps remove the rank contribution inside the preliminary polynomial estimate.
- **claim-uniform-klt-character-exponent** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-characters)): For integers $n\geq0$ and $m\geq1$, there is an integer $L(n,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $(X,\Delta)$ be an integral projective klt $n$-fold pair, with $\Delta\geq0$ and $m(K_X+\Delta)\sim0$ as an integral principal divisor. Choose a rational $m$-canonical form $\theta$ with $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\theta)=-m\Delta$. For every B-birational self-map $f$ of $(X,\Delta)$, write $f^*\theta=c\theta$, where $c\in\mathbb C^*$. Then $c^{L(n,m)}=1$.
  - Explanation: Crepant birational self-maps act on the prescribed log trivializing form through roots of unity killed by a common exponent depending only on dimension and the fixed degree. The self-map itself need not have finite order.
- **claim-terminal-action-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-char-terminal-model)): In the setting of Theorem <a href="#thm:characters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:characters">17</a>, there is a projective crepant birational morphism $$(V,B_V)\longrightarrow(X,\Delta)$$ such that $V$ is $\mathbb Q$-factorial, $B_V\geq0$, and the pair $(V,B_V)$ is terminal. Every B-birational self-map of $(X,\Delta)$ induces a pseudo-automorphism of $V$ preserving $B_V$.
  - Explanation: A crepant terminal pair model converts each B-birational action into a boundary-preserving pseudo-automorphism, hence an isomorphism outside subsets of codimension at least two.
- **claim-quasi-etale-product-input** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-char-product-input)): Let $(V,B_V)$ be an integral projective klt pair with $B_V\geq0$, $K_V$ rational Cartier, and $K_V+B_V\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$. There is a finite quasi-étale cover $$P=\prod_{i\in I}P_i\longrightarrow V$$ whose positive-dimensional factors have the following properties. There is at most one abelian factor and at most one rationally connected factor $(F,H)$, where $(F,H)$ is klt, $H\geq0$, and $K_F+H\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$; the entire pulled-back boundary is the pullback of $H$, and is zero if that factor is absent. All factors are $\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein. All other factors are irreducible Calabi–Yau or irreducible holomorphic symplectic varieties. They, and every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of them, have canonical singularities and Cartier trivial canonical class. Their reflexive form algebra is generated by a top form in the Calabi–Yau case and by a symplectic form in the symplectic case. In particular they have no reflexive one-forms or vector fields.* *The cover can be chosen so that every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of $F$ is rationally connected. On every such cover, reflexive one-forms and vector fields tangent to the pulled-back boundary vanish.
  - Explanation: Under the integral projective klt, effective boundary, rational Cartier canonical class, and rational log triviality hypotheses, a quasi-étale product cover isolates the boundary on a rationally connected factor. Further-cover properties control the remaining form algebras.
- **claim-lift-and-separate-factor-actions** (construction, [source](#stmt-char-factor-actions)): Let $(V,B_V)$ be as in Lemma <a href="#char:terminal-model" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="char:terminal-model">18</a>, of dimension $n>0$. Choose a product cover $P=\prod_{i\in I}P_i\to V$ as in Input <a href="#char:product-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="char:product-input">19</a>, and let $f$ be a B-birational self-map with $f^*\theta=c\theta$. There are normal integral projective varieties $R,R_i$, finite quasi-étale maps $$R\xrightarrow{\ \psi\ }\prod_{i\in I}R_i \longrightarrow\prod_{i\in I}P_i\longrightarrow V,$$ and birational self-maps $h_i$ of $R_i$ with the following properties. Each $R_i\to P_i$ is a finite quasi-étale cover. Write $H_i$ for its boundary, zero except on the rationally connected factor, and put $p_i=m$ on that factor and $p_i=1$ on the others. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\theta_i$ with $$\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\theta_i)=-p_iH_i,\qquad h_i^*\theta_i=d_i\theta_i \quad(d_i\in\mathbb C^*),$$ and $$\label{char:scalar-product} c^{n!}=\prod_{i\in I}d_i^{m/p_i}.$$ In particular each $h_i$ is B-birational for $(R_i,H_i)$.
  - Explanation: A cover admitting a lift and preservation of factor directions produce birational actions on factor covers. The global character power equals the product of factor characters with the stated pluricanonical weights; no bound on the cover degree is required.
- **claim-rationally-connected-character-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-char-rc-bound)): For $r,m\geq1$ there is an integer $E_{\mathrm{rc}}(r,m)>0$ that kills the degree-$m$ B-birational character of every integral projective rationally connected klt pair $(F,H)$ of dimension $r$ with $H\geq0$ and $m(K_F+H)\sim0$ integral principal.
  - Explanation: Boundedness modulo flops and a bounded-family pluricanonical representation theorem give a common exponent for the rationally connected factor with fixed principal log trivializing degree.
- **claim-boundary-free-character-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-char-hodge-bound)): For each $r\geq1$ there is an integer $E_0(r)>0$ with the following property. Let $Y$ be a connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of an $r$-dimensional abelian, irreducible Calabi–Yau, or irreducible holomorphic symplectic factor of Input <a href="#char:product-input" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="char:product-input">19</a>. Every birational self-map of $Y$ acts on its canonical volume form by a scalar killed by $E_0(r)$.
  - Explanation: The specified boundary-free factor covers carry an integral polarized top-form Hodge lattice of bounded rank. Their volume-form characters are roots of unity with bounded cyclotomic degree and a common annihilating exponent.
- **claim-within-pair-residue-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-res-within)): Let $(Y,\Gamma)$ and $\theta$ satisfy <a href="#res:form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="res:form">[res:form]</a>. For any two minimal strata $A,A'$, there is a B-birational map $\phi:(A,\Gamma_A)\dashrightarrow(A',\Gamma_{A'})$ such that $$\phi^*\theta_{A'}=\theta_A.$$
  - Explanation: Within a projective integral dlt pair with the prescribed even-degree form, minimal strata admit a crepant birational comparison preserving residues exactly.
- **claim-across-pair-residue-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-res-across)): Let $(S,\Delta_S)$ and $(T,\Delta_T)$ be projective integral dlt pairs of the same dimension, with rational $m$-canonical forms satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\theta_S)=-m\Delta_S,\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\theta_T)=-m\Delta_T.$$ Suppose $\tau:S\dashrightarrow T$ is birational and $\tau^*\theta_T=r\theta_S$ for $r\in\mathbb C^*$. Then there are minimal strata $A\subset S$ and $A'\subset T$ and a B-birational map $\psi:(A,\Delta_A)\dashrightarrow(A',\Delta_{A'})$ such that $$\label{res:across-identity} \psi^*\theta_{A'}=r\theta_A.$$
  - Explanation: A birational comparison multiplying the ambient log forms by a scalar induces a crepant comparison between suitable minimal strata with exactly the same scalar. Birationality and even degree remove the apparent normal-coordinate determinant factor.

        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a compact Kähler manifold of complex dimension $2n$, where $n\\ge1$, let $D$ be a Kähler form, and let $H$ be a smooth closed semipositive $(1,1)$-form of pointwise rank at most $n$. If $z\\in H^{n,n}(M)$ satisfies $[H]^n z=0$, then, for every $R\\ge1$, $$\\label{hodge:gap}\n \\lVert z\\rVert_{D+RH}\n \\le R^{(n-\\delta_n)/2}\\lVert z\\rVert_{D+H},\n \\qquad\n \\delta_n=\\frac{1}{\\binom{2n}{n}+2}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "Fix an integer $n\\ge2$ and a real number $c>0$. Let $X$ be a normal projective complex $n$-fold with canonical singularities and $K_X\\sim0$ as an integral principal divisor.         "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-rank",
        "exact_quote": "For every integer $n\\ge2$ there is a constant $R_n$ with the following property. Let $T$ be a projective $\\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal complex $n$-fold with $K_T\\sim0$. Suppose that a smooth projective resolution $U$ has $h^1(U,\\mathcal O_U)=0$, and that every positive-dimensional rational image of $T$ of dimension less than $n$ has a rationally connected smooth projective resolution.         "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-input-rank-chains",
        "exact_quote": "A finite nonempty collection of marked covering families determines a rational fibration $\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. If its generic fibre, called a leaf, has dimension $h$, then $1\\le h\\le\\dim V$, every generic member lies in a leaf, and at most $h$ generic movements reach a point generic in the leaf over the algebraic closure of the field of its starting mark. Suppose that, for each family, either $$\\gamma(P|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\\le B,\n \\qquad\\text{or}\\qquad\n \\dim C_b=1,\\quad\n \\frac{P\\cdot C_b}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{mult}}_{u(b)}C_b}\\le B.$$ For a smooth model $F$ of the geometric generic leaf, with $P$ pulled back, one then has $$\\label{eq:rank:chain-bound}\n                  \\gamma(P|_F;F)\\le hB,\n                  \\qquad (P|_F)^h\\le(hB)^h.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-input-rank-tracking",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt of dimension $d\\ge2$, and let $N$ be big, nef and semiample. If $d+1$ equally spaced positive rational levels, with spacing $\\Delta>0$, lie strictly between $(N^d)^{1/d}$ and $\\gamma(N;Z)$, there is a covering family of subvarieties $W\\subset Z$, of dimension $0<e<d$, such that a smooth geometric generic model $W^*$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n N^eW&\\le(2/\\Delta)^{d-e}N^d,\\label{eq:rank:tracking-degree}\\\\\n K_{W^*}(N|_{W^*})^{e-1}\n &\\le\\bigl(K_Z+(2d/\\Delta)N\\bigr)|_W(N|_W)^{e-1}.\n \\label{eq:rank:tracking-canonical}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If a smooth model of $Z$ has nonnegative Kodaira dimension, so does $W^*$.         "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $e\\ge1$ and $C'>0$.           "exact_quote": "For every normal projective canonical $V$ birational to $T$ with $K_V\\sim0$, and every ample rational Cartier divisor $P$ on $V$, there is a marked covering family of subvarieties $C_b$ with $0<\\dim C_b<n$, whose smooth geometric generic models satisfy $$\\label{eq:rank:low-order}\n \\kappa(C_b^*)=0,\n \\qquad\n \\gamma(P|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\\le\\eta(P^n)^{1/n},$$ where the mark is smooth on the member.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.2"
        }
      ],

    },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-calibration",
        "exact_quote": "Let $h:X\\to Y$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, where $X$ is a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial canonical $n$-fold with $K_X\\sim0$ and irregularity zero on a resolution. Suppose that $0<b:=\\dim Y<n$ and that no prime divisor of $X$ maps into a subset of codimension at least two in $Y$. Let $\\widehat Y$ be a normal projective variety isomorphic to $Y$ in codimension one, and let $H$ be a very ample divisor on $\\widehat Y$ satisfying $$H^b\\le N_0,\n \\qquad\n \\bigl|K_{\\widehat Y}\\cdot H^{b-1}\\bigr|\\le N_0.$$ Pullback from the common smooth open subsets of the bases defines $$W=h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cl}}(Y)_{\\mathbb R}\n   =h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cl}}(\\widehat Y)_{\\mathbb R}\n   \\subset N^1(X)_{\\mathbb R},\n \\qquad\n \\ell(h^*L)=L\\cdot H^{b-1}.$$ Let $A=h^*H$, represented by an effective integral Weil divisor. There is a linear extension $\\ell_X:N^1(X)_{\\mathbb R}\\to\\mathbb R$ of $\\ell$, nonnegative on the pseudo-effective cone, and there are a rational number $\\delta>0$ and a big rational divisor $D$ such that $$\\label{eq:rank:calibration}\n c\\delta\\le v(D)\\le C\\delta,\n \\qquad\n D-\\frac{\\delta}{3}A\\text{ is big},\n \\qquad\n \\ell_X(D)\\le C\\delta.$$ Here $v(D)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(D)^{1/n}$, $c>0$ depends only on $n$, and $C$ depends only on $n,N_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-characters",
        "exact_quote": "For integers $n\\geq0$ and $m\\geq1$, there is an integer $L(n,m)>0$ with the following property. Let $(X,\\Delta)$ be an integral projective klt $n$-fold pair, with $\\Delta\\geq0$ and $m(K_X+\\Delta)\\sim0$ as an integral principal divisor. Choose a rational $m$-canonical form $\\theta$ with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}(\\theta)=-m\\Delta$. For every B-birational self-map $f$ of $(X,\\Delta)$, write $f^*\\theta=c\\theta$, where $c\\in\\mathbb C^*$. Then $c^{L(n,m)}=1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-char-product-input",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B_V)$ be an integral projective klt pair with $B_V\\geq0$, $K_V$ rational Cartier, and $K_V+B_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. There is a finite quasi-étale cover $$P=\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\\longrightarrow V$$ whose positive-dimensional factors have the following properties. There is at most one abelian factor and at most one rationally connected factor $(F,H)$, where $(F,H)$ is klt, $H\\geq0$, and $K_F+H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$; the entire pulled-back boundary is the pullback of $H$, and is zero if that factor is absent. All factors are $\\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein. All other factors are irreducible Calabi–Yau or irreducible holomorphic symplectic varieties. They, and every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of them, have canonical singularities and Cartier trivial canonical class. Their reflexive form algebra is generated by a top form in the Calabi–Yau case and by a symplectic form in the symplectic case. In particular they have no reflexive one-forms or vector fields.*\n\n*The cover can be chosen so that every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of $F$ is rationally connected. On every such cover, reflexive one-forms and vector fields tangent to the pulled-back boundary vanish.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-char-product-input",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B_V)$ be an integral projective klt pair with $B_V\\geq0$, $K_V$ rational Cartier, and $K_V+B_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$. There is at most one abelian factor and at most one rationally connected factor $(F,H)$, where $(F,H)$ is klt, $H\\geq0$, and $K_F+H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$; the entire pulled-back boundary is the pullback of $H$, and is zero if that factor is absent. All factors are $\\mathbb Q$-Gorenstein. All other factors are irreducible Calabi–Yau or irreducible holomorphic symplectic varieties. They, and every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of them, have canonical singularities and Cartier trivial canonical class. Their reflexive form algebra is generated by a top form in the Calabi–Yau case and by a symplectic form in the symplectic case. In particular they have no reflexive one-forms or vector fields.*\n\n*The cover can be chosen so that every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of $F$ is rationally connected. On every such cover, reflexive one-forms and vector fields tangent to the pulled-back boundary vanish.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.2"

      "exact_statement": "Let $(V,B_V)$ be an integral projective klt pair with $B_V\\geq0$, $K_V$ rational Cartier, and $K_V+B_V\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$.       "explanation": "Under the integral projective klt, effective boundary, rational Cartier canonical class, and rational log triviality hypotheses, a quasi-étale product cover isolates the boundary on a rationally connected factor. Further-cover properties control the remaining form algebras.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-char-factor-actions",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B_V)$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#char:terminal-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"char:terminal-model\">18</a>, of dimension $n>0$. Choose a product cover $P=\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\\to V$ as in Input <a href=\"#char:product-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"char:product-input\">19</a>, and let $f$ be a B-birational self-map with $f^*\\theta=c\\theta$. There are normal integral projective varieties $R,R_i$, finite quasi-étale maps $$R\\xrightarrow{\\ \\psi\\ }\\prod_{i\\in I}R_i\n     \\longrightarrow\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\\longrightarrow V,$$ and birational self-maps $h_i$ of $R_i$ with the following properties. Each $R_i\\to P_i$ is a finite quasi-étale cover. Write $H_i$ for its boundary, zero except on the rationally connected factor, and put $p_i=m$ on that factor and $p_i=1$ on the others. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\\theta_i$ with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}(\\theta_i)=-p_iH_i,\\qquad h_i^*\\theta_i=d_i\\theta_i\n \\quad(d_i\\in\\mathbb C^*),$$ and $$\\label{char:scalar-product}\n                       c^{n!}=\\prod_{i\\in I}d_i^{m/p_i}.$$ In particular each $h_i$ is B-birational for $(R_i,H_i)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-034.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-char-factor-actions",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B_V)$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#char:terminal-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"char:terminal-model\">18</a>, of dimension $n>0$. Choose a product cover $P=\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\\to V$ as in Input <a href=\"#char:product-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"char:product-input\">19</a>, and let $f$ be a B-birational self-map with $f^*\\theta=c\\theta$. <!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Demi-normal scheme** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A scheme is *demi-normal* if it is reduced, satisfies Serre’s condition $S_2$, is seminormal, and has only nodes in codimension one.
- **Semi-log-canonical pair** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The pair $(X,B)$ is *semi-log-canonical*, abbreviated slc, if $K_X+B$ is rational Cartier and $$(\bar X,\bar C+\nu_*^{-1}B)$$ is log canonical.
- **Log Calabi–Yau pair** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We call $(X,B)$ a log Calabi–Yau pair when, in addition, $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$: some positive Cartier multiple has trivial associated line bundle.
- **Index of a log Calabi–Yau pair** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *index* of the pair is the least such positive integer.
- **Integral principal triviality convention** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For normal varieties, $m(K_X+\Delta)\sim0$ means that this is an *integral principal divisor*.
- **Rational pluricanonical form** ([source](#eq:form-trivialization)): A rational $m$-canonical form is a nonzero element of $(\bigwedge^n\Omega_{F/\mathbb C})^{\otimes m}$.
- **Divisor of a rational pluricanonical form** ([source](#eq:form-trivialization)): If $\eta$ is a rational top form and $\theta=u\eta^{\otimes m}$, put $$\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\theta)=\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(u)+m\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}(\eta).$$
- **B-birational map** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A birational map between pairs is *$B$-birational*, or *crepant birational*, if the log canonical pullbacks agree on a common resolution, with canonical divisors chosen compatibly.
- **Pseudo-automorphism** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A *pseudo-automorphism* is a birational self-map which is an isomorphism on open subsets with complements of codimension at least two.
- **Contraction** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Recall that a *contraction* $h:V\to Y$ is a projective surjective morphism of normal varieties with $h_*\mathcal O_V=\mathcal O_Y$.
- **Volume of a big rational divisor** ([source](#pm-h-9)): For a big rational divisor $D$ on an $n$-fold, write $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(D)=\limsup_{m\to\infty}n!h^0(mD)/m^n$, with degrees divisible enough to make the divisor integral.
- **Top-form rational Hodge substructure and rank** ([source](#sec:hodge)): For a smooth projective complex $n$-fold $U$, let $$T(U)\subset H^n(U,\mathbb Q)$$ be the smallest rational Hodge substructure whose complexification contains $H^{n,0}(U)$, and put $r(U)=\dim_\mathbb QT(U)$.
- **Hodge norm** ([source](#sec:hodge)): We use the Hodge norm $\lVert z\rVert_\omega$ of a cohomology class on a compact Kähler manifold: this is the $L^2$-norm of its $\omega$-harmonic representative.
- **Seshadri constant** ([source](#sec:diagonal)): For an ample rational divisor $h$ on a projective variety and a smooth point $x$, its Seshadri constant is $$\epsilon(h;x)=\inf_{C\ni x}\frac{h\cdot C}{\mathop{\mathrm{mult}}_x C},$$ where the infimum runs over integral curves through $x$.
- **Double-dominant subvariety** ([source](#sec:diagonal)): A subvariety of any such model will be called *double dominant* if its maps to both copies of $U$ are dominant.
- **Effective comparison of rational divisors** ([source](#sec:rank)): We write $D_1\preceq D_2$ when $D_2-D_1$ is rationally linearly equivalent to an effective rational divisor; such a comparison is restricted only to subvarieties not contained in the chosen effective difference.
- **Complete-system section order** ([source](#eq:rank:order-volume)): At a smooth point $x$, set $$\gamma(P;V,x)= \sup_{\substack{m>0\text{ sufficiently divisible}\\ 0\ne s\in H^0(\widetilde V,m\mu^*P)}} \frac{\mathop{\mathrm{ord}}_x(s)}m,$$ where $\mu:\widetilde V\to V$ is a smooth projective resolution that is an isomorphism near $x$.
- **Marked covering family** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A *marked covering family* consists of an integral parameter space $S$, a dominant mark map $u:S\to V$, and an incidence $\mathcal I\subset S\times V$ whose geometric generic fibre $C_b$ is integral, positive-dimensional, and contains $u(b)$.
- **Attained subfield** ([source](#pm-h-19)): Call a subfield of $\mathbb C(T)$ *attained* if it is the field of a contraction $T'\to Y$, where $T'$ is $\mathbb Q$-factorial terminal and is equipped with a crepant birational map to $T$ that is an isomorphism in codimension one.
- **Quasi-étale morphism** ([source](#pm-h-27)): A finite surjective morphism of normal varieties is *quasi-étale* if it is étale in codimension one.
- **Dlt stratum and minimal stratum** ([source](#sec:residues)): For a dlt pair $(Y,\Gamma)$, a *stratum* means $Y$ itself or an lc centre. A minimal stratum is minimal for inclusion; thus it is $Y$ if the pair is klt.
- **Logarithmic residue form** ([source](#res:form)): Its logarithmic residue is the rational $m$-canonical form $$\theta_W=f\|_W\,(dy_1\wedge\cdots\wedge dy_{\dim W})^{\otimes m}.$$

          "source_id": "PM-034.2"

| q-hodge-gap-deficit | \delta_n | = | $\frac{1}{\binom{2n}{n}+2}$ | The deficit depends only on the dimension parameter. |

| q-diagonal-self-pairing | (-1)^n\int_{\widetilde P}v\overline v | = | $\frac{l^2}{r}$ | The identity uses the polarization degree and the top-form Hodge rank. |
| q-uniform-top-form-rank | r(T) | ≤ | $R_n$ | The bound depends only on dimension. |
| q-section-order-volume-lower-bound | \gamma(P;V) | ≥ | $(P^d)^{1/d}$ | The exponent is determined by the variety dimension. |
| q-chain-leaf-order | \gamma(P\|_F;F) | ≤ | $hB$ | The leaf dimension multiplies the member order or curve-ratio bound. |
| q-chain-leaf-volume | (P\|_F)^h | ≤ | $(hB)^h$ | The volume bound is the leaf-order bound raised to the leaf dimension. |
| q-low-order-member-bound | \gamma(P\|_{C_b};C_b,u(b)) | ≤ | $\eta(P^n)^{1/n}$ | The rank threshold depends on dimension and the desired normalized order. |
| q-calibration-volume-upper | v(D) | ≤ | $C\delta$ | The upper constant depends only on dimension and the base degree bounds. |
| q-calibration-volume-lower | v(D) | ≥ | $c\delta$ | The positive lower constant depends only on dimension. |
| q-calibrated-functional-degree | \ell_X(D) | ≤ | $C\delta$ | The bound shares the calibration parameter controlling the volume root. |
| q-semistable-sheaf-sections | h^0(\mathbb P^b,\mathcal G) | ≤ | $C_bu\bigl(1+\max\{0,\mu\}\bigr)^b$ | The constant depends only on the projective-space dimension; rank enters linearly. |
| q-section-bound-explicit-prefactor | C_b | = | $b^b/b!$ | A permissible constant depending only on the base dimension. |
| q-factor-character-product | c^{n!} | = | $\prod_{i\in I}d_i^{m/p_i}$ | The weights retain the prescribed pluricanonical degrees on the factors. |
| q-rationally-connected-character-exponent | E_{\mathrm{rc}}(r,m) | = | $kN!$ | The bounded-family representation supplies the auxiliary degree and image-order bound. |
| q-abelian-middle-cohomology | \dim_{\mathbb Q}T(U) | ≤ | $\binom{2r}{r}$ | The middle Betti number depends only on the abelian dimension. |
| q-cyclotomic-degree-bound | \varphi(e) | ≤ | $b(r)$ | The factor dimension controls the Hodge rank bound. |
| q-uniform-character-exponent | L(n,m) | = | $n!E(n,m)$ | The common factor exponent depends only on dimension and the fixed degree. |
| q-uniform-slc-trivializing-degree | a | = | $mL$ | The character exponents range over the dimensions of proper minimal strata. |

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-calibration",
          "exact_quote": "Let $h:X\\to Y$ be a contraction of normal projective complex varieties, where $X$ is a $\\mathbb Q$-factorial canonical $n$-fold with $K_X\\sim0$ and irregularity zero on a resolution. Suppose that $0<b:=\\dim Y<n$ and that no prime divisor of $X$ maps into a subset of codimension at least two in $Y$. Let $\\widehat Y$ be a normal projective variety isomorphic to $Y$ in codimension one, and let $H$ be a very ample divisor on $\\widehat Y$ satisfying $$H^b\\le N_0,\n \\qquad\n \\bigl|K_{\\widehat Y}\\cdot H^{b-1}\\bigr|\\le N_0.$$ Pullback from the common smooth open subsets of the bases defines $$W=h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cl}}(Y)_{\\mathbb R}\n   =h^*\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cl}}(\\widehat Y)_{\\mathbb R}\n   \\subset N^1(X)_{\\mathbb R},\n \\qquad\n \\ell(h^*L)=L\\cdot H^{b-1}.$$ Let $A=h^*H$, represented by an effective integral Weil divisor. There is a linear extension $\\ell_X:N^1(X)_{\\mathbb R}\\to\\mathbb R$ of $\\ell$, nonnegative on the pseudo-effective cone, and there are a rational number $\\delta>0$ and a big rational divisor $D$ such that $$\\label{eq:rank:calibration}\n c\\delta\\le v(D)\\le C\\delta,\n \\qquad\n D-\\frac{\\delta}{3}A\\text{ is big},\n \\qquad\n \\ell_X(D)\\le C\\delta.$$ Here $v(D)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}(D)^{1/n}$, $c>0$ depends only on $n$, and $C$ depends only on $n,N_0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.2"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-char-factor-actions",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(V,B_V)$ be as in Lemma <a href=\"#char:terminal-model\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"char:terminal-model\">18</a>, of dimension $n>0$. Choose a product cover $P=\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\\to V$ as in Input <a href=\"#char:product-input\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"char:product-input\">19</a>, and let $f$ be a B-birational self-map with $f^*\\theta=c\\theta$. There are normal integral projective varieties $R,R_i$, finite quasi-étale maps $$R\\xrightarrow{\\ \\psi\\ }\\prod_{i\\in I}R_i\n     \\longrightarrow\\prod_{i\\in I}P_i\\longrightarrow V,$$ and birational self-maps $h_i$ of $R_i$ with the following properties. Each $R_i\\to P_i$ is a finite quasi-étale cover. Write $H_i$ for its boundary, zero except on the rationally connected factor, and put $p_i=m$ on that factor and $p_i=1$ on the others. There are rational $p_i$-canonical forms $\\theta_i$ with $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}(\\theta_i)=-p_iH_i,\\qquad h_i^*\\theta_i=d_i\\theta_i\n \\quad(d_i\\in\\mathbb C^*),$$ and $$\\label{char:scalar-product}\n                       c^{n!}=\\prod_{i\\in I}d_i^{m/p_i}.$$ In particular each $h_i$ is B-birational for $(R_i,H_i)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.2"
        }
      ],

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-crepant-form-criterion** [claim](#stmt-lem-form-crepant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-form-crepant): Compare the two pulled-back forms on a common resolution and use constancy of a rational function with zero divisor. -> Equivalence between crepant birationality and scalar proportionality.
- **step-normal-index-input** [claim](#stmt-input-normal-index) / [proof](#stmt-input-normal-index): Import the companion normal log canonical index theorem. -> A common integral principal log canonical multiple on normal pairs.
- **step-good-model-input** [claim](#stmt-input-good-model) / [proof](#stmt-input-good-model): Import the all-dimensional good log minimal model theorem. -> A good minimal model with semiample transformed adjoint.
- **step-bounded-comparison-base** [claim](#stmt-input-bounded-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-input-bounded-base): Apply the canonical bundle formula and bounded generalized torsion result, then use bounded projective embeddings and curve adjunction. -> A bounded comparison base with bounded polarization and canonical degrees.
- **step-hodge-birational-invariance** [claim](#stmt-hodge-basic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hodge-basic): Use pullback, pushforward, and minimality of the rational Hodge substructure generated by top forms. -> Birational invariance, primitivity, and vanishing through lower-dimensional images.
- **step-hodge-norm-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-hodge-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hodge-gap): Differentiate the norm with the harmonic representative fixed, estimate a pointwise deficit, and exploit orthogonality of harmonic and exact top-degree forms. -> A dimension-dependent strict loss in Hodge norm growth.
- **step-diagonal-rank-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diagonal): Control diagonal vanishing and double-dominant base components, construct an ample class with invisible corrections, and compare a fixed pairing with the Hodge norm gap. -> A top-form Hodge rank bound depending on dimension and local positivity.
- **step-generic-chain-input** [claim](#stmt-input-rank-chains) / [proof](#stmt-input-rank-chains): Import the generic-chain fibration and order-propagation construction. -> Geometrically integral leaves with bounded order and volume.
- **step-tracking-input** [claim](#stmt-input-rank-tracking) / [proof](#stmt-input-rank-tracking): Import tracking from separated section-order levels, including canonical-degree and Kodaira-dimension inheritance. -> Intermediate-dimensional covering members with controlled degrees.
- **step-small-iitaka-fibre-input** [claim](#stmt-input-rank-small-fibre) / [proof](#stmt-input-rank-small-fibre): Import the small-volume Iitaka-fibre reduction. -> Positive-dimensional Kodaira-dimension-zero fibres with vanishing adjoint volume.
- **step-low-order-families** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-low-order) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-low-order): Argue by contradiction: small Seshadri constants force large ambient orders, then tracking, Iitaka fibres, and decreasing dimension produce small complete-system orders. -> Covering members of Kodaira dimension zero with small normalized order.
- **step-attain-chain-fields** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-attainment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-attainment): Identify the chain field with the section-ratio field of a movable divisor and realize it by a semiample contraction after a small crepant minimal model program. -> A proper chain field attained on a terminal model.
- **step-calibrate-big-divisor** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-calibration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-calibration): Separate the big cone from a minimizing affine half-space, then use volume continuity, divisorial Zariski decomposition, and effective birationality to calibrate a rational big divisor. -> An extended nonnegative degree functional and a divisor with comparable volume and degree scales.
- **step-linear-rank-section-count** [claim](#stmt-lem-rank-section-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-section-count): Combine a general-line splitting estimate and flag restrictions with finite power-map pullbacks, then pass to the limit to remove the rank loss. -> A polynomial slope section estimate linear in sheaf rank.
- **step-complete-uniform-rank** [claim](#stmt-thm-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rank-section-count-2): Choose a maximal attained base, force low-order families to be vertical, bound fibre spans by injective jets, derive horizontal slope bounds, and contradict volume growth. -> A dimension-only bound for the top-form Hodge rank.
- **step-terminalize-action** [claim](#stmt-char-terminal-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-terminal-model): Extract the finitely many divisorial places with nonpositive discrepancy and distinguish divisors on the terminal model from exceptional valuations by form orders. -> A terminal model on which every B-birational self-map is a boundary-preserving pseudo-automorphism.
- **step-product-cover-input** [claim](#stmt-char-product-input) / [proof](#stmt-char-product-input): Import the cover-stable structural decomposition into rationally connected, abelian, and irreducible boundary-free factors. -> A quasi-étale product cover with the stated form and tangent-field vanishing properties.
- **step-lift-factor-actions** [claim](#stmt-char-factor-actions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-factor-actions): Use a characteristic finite-index cover to lift the action, central tangent idempotents to preserve factor directions after a uniform power, and derivation constants to recover factor fields. -> B-birational factor actions and the weighted character-product identity.
- **step-bound-rationally-connected-character** [claim](#stmt-char-rc-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-rc-bound): Pass to bounded models modulo flops, bound boundary support degrees and coefficient choices, and apply bounded-family representation finiteness. -> A common factor character exponent depending on dimension and degree.
- **step-bound-boundary-free-character** [claim](#stmt-char-hodge-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-hodge-bound): Bound the top-form Hodge rank, preserve its integral polarized lattice under birational pull-push, and apply Kronecker's theorem and the cyclotomic-degree bound. -> A dimension-dependent exponent killing canonical volume-form characters.
- **step-assemble-klt-character-exponent** [claim](#stmt-thm-characters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-char-hodge-bound-2): Take a common multiple of the factor exponents and combine it with the factor-direction permutation power. -> A uniform exponent for every B-birational log volume-form character.
- **step-compare-residues-within** [claim](#stmt-res-within) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-within): Apply linking and even-degree residue compatibility between minimal dlt strata. -> A crepant comparison preserving the residue exactly.
- **step-compare-residues-across** [claim](#stmt-res-across) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-across): Construct corresponding maximal-codimension strata on a common resolution; compare monomial valuations to prove unimodularity and equality of stratum function fields. -> A minimal-stratum birational comparison retaining the original scalar.
- **step-descend-uniform-slc-trivialization** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-res-across-2): Use normalization forms and conductor comparisons to identify cycle products with klt characters, kill them uniformly, rescale along spanning trees, extend by the Serre condition, and descend from the complex field. -> A uniform principal Cartier multiple on the original semi-log-canonical pair.

          "exact_quote": "A finite nonempty collection of marked covering families determines a rational fibration $\\xi:V\\dashrightarrow M$ with geometrically integral generic fibre. If its generic fibre, called a leaf, has dimension $h$, then $1\\le h\\le\\dim V$, every generic member lies in a leaf, and at most $h$ generic movements reach a point generic in the leaf over the algebraic closure of the field of its starting mark. Suppose that, for each family, either $$\\gamma(P|_{C_b};C_b,u(b))\\le B,\n \\qquad\\text{or}\\qquad\n \\dim C_b=1,\\quad\n \\frac{P\\cdot C_b}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{mult}}_{u(b)}C_b}\\le B.$$ For a smooth model $F$ of the geometric generic leaf, with $P$ pulled back, one then has $$\\label{eq:rank:chain-bound}\n                  \\gamma(P|_F;F)\\le hB,\n                  \\qquad (P|_F)^h\\le(hB)^h.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-034.2"

          "exact_quote": "Let $Z$ be projective, $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and klt of dimension $d\\ge2$, and let $N$ be big, nef and semiample. If $d+1$ equally spaced positive rational levels, with spacing $\\Delta>0$, lie strictly between $(N^d)^{1/d}$ and $\\gamma(N;Z)$, there is a covering family of subvarieties $W\\subset Z$, of dimension $0<e<d$, such that a smooth geometric generic model $W^*$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n N^eW&\\le(2/\\Delta)^{d-e}N^d,\\label{eq:rank:tracking-degree}\\\\\n K_{W^*}(N|_{W^*})^{e-1}\n &\\le\\bigl(K_Z+(2d/\\Delta)N\\bigr)|_W(N|_W)^{e-1}.\n \\label{eq:rank:tracking-canonical}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If a smooth model of $Z$ has nonnegative Kodaira dimension, so does $W^*$. The same inheritance statement holds for arbitrary covering families.",
          "source_id": "PM-034.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniform-slc-trivialization**: For the specified semi-log-canonical log Calabi–Yau pairs, a common multiple makes the log canonical divisor Cartier and its associated line bundle trivial. Its dependence is restricted to dimension and the fixed finite rational coefficient set.
- **invariant-hodge-norm-gap**: The stated cup-product vanishing forces a strict deficit in Hodge norm growth under the specified semipositive metric stretching.
- **invariant-uniform-top-form-rank**: The rational Hodge structure generated by holomorphic top forms has rank bounded solely by dimension under the stated terminality, irregularity, and rational-image hypotheses.
- **invariant-uniform-klt-character**: Every B-birational self-map acts on the prescribed trivializing form by a root of unity annihilated by a common exponent depending only on dimension and the trivializing degree.
- **invariant-residue-multiplier-preservation**: A birational comparison of the prescribed forms on dlt pairs induces a B-birational comparison of suitable minimal strata with exactly the same scalar multiplier.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-unrestricted-boundary-coefficients**: Apply the uniform slc multiple to arbitrary real boundaries or an unrestricted coefficient collection. -> The asserted uniform dependence does not cover that boundary regime.
- **anti-rationally-trivial-multiple-as-principal**: Treat rational linear triviality of a proposed multiple as an actual Cartier trivialization in that same degree. -> The rational statement alone does not provide the integral principal divisor required for the form identities and fixed-degree character theorem.
- **anti-character-bound-as-map-order**: Infer a uniform finite order for the birational transformation from its character exponent. -> An annihilated scalar character does not bound the order of the transformation itself.
- **anti-unconditional-hodge-rank-bound**: Apply the dimension-only Hodge rank bound to every variety with trivial canonical class. -> The rank theorem also imposes terminality, vanishing irregularity, and restrictions on smaller rational images.
- **anti-odd-degree-residue-matching**: Use equality of branch residues as the nodal descent rule without checking degree parity. -> The nodal residue rule includes a degree-dependent sign; the exact residue comparisons in this section use even degree.
- **anti-finite-graph-check-as-index-certificate**: Certify the full uniform index theorem by checking residue compatibility on a supplied finite graph. -> The graph calculation assumes that the geometric character bounds already annihilate every cycle obstruction. A finite graph check cannot establish those uniform bounds or the extension of the descended generator over the variety.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main theorem asserts existence of a uniform trivializing multiple over an unrestricted geometric class. Its character exponent depends on a uniform Hodge rank bound whose proof explicitly states: "The proof proceeds by contradiction." The finite spanning-tree construction applies only after the geometric character exponents have been supplied; it does not compute those bounds or certify the geometric hypotheses and global Cartier descent. Consequently a finite executable oracle would test only a conditional fragment, not the stated uniform theorem.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform index theorem establishes existence of a common trivializing multiple but supplies no effective procedure for computing that multiple from a software representation of a pair. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The metric estimate concerns Hodge norms of cohomology classes on compact Kähler manifolds. It supplies no discrete evolution rule or simulation error contract. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The characterization of crepant birational maps requires prescribed rational canonical forms and normal projective pairs. It does not supply a general symbolic decision procedure for recognizing such maps. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The residue descent proof contains a finite graph construction: once exact powered edge ratios are supplied and their products around closed walks are trivial, spanning trees determine compatible vertex scalings. Computing the geometric exponent and the residue ratios remains outside this conditional construction. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The conductor graph records normalization components and generic nodes, including multiple edges and loops. The paper uses this representation in a descent argument without establishing a new data structure or storage invariant. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Hodge rank bound controls the dimension of a rational cohomological substructure under geometric hypotheses. It does not bound hardware resources or expose a parallel computational kernel. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The character theorem bounds scalars arising from crepant birational self-maps of klt pairs. It gives no cryptographic hardness assumption, adversarial model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The section estimate concerns torsion-free slope-semistable sheaves on complex projective space. Its rank and slope are geometric quantities, with no supplied learning model or statistical generalization contract. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The calibration proof minimizes a functional on a geometric cone and uses separation and continuity. It provides an existence argument without a finite encoding of the feasible set or an optimization algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Matching conductor residues depends on local nodal geometry and canonical generators. These conditions supply no communication, fault, or concurrency semantics for distributed replicas. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results have direct foundational significance for algebraic geometry, but existence of good minimal models does not itself provide a finite engineering contract for constructing those models from arbitrary input equations. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-main-geometric-regime** (hypothesis): The uniform index conclusion requires the connected, projective, equidimensional semi-log-canonical log Calabi–Yau regime and the stated dimension range. Blocked use: ['Apply the main theorem to an arbitrary nonnormal scheme or a nonprojective pair.', 'Extend the stated dimension range without invoking the separate lower-dimensional theorem.']
- **limit-fixed-finite-rational-coefficients** (hypothesis): The common multiple depends on a fixed finite rational coefficient set; the main theorem does not assert the same uniformity for unrestricted real boundaries or arbitrary infinite coefficient collections. Blocked use: ['Use the same asserted common multiple after removing the finite rational coefficient restriction.']
- **limit-characteristic-zero** (formal_scope): The ground field is algebraically closed of characteristic zero. The complex analytic argument and subsequent field descent do not establish a positive-characteristic or mixed-characteristic analogue. Blocked use: ['Apply the index theorem over a finite field or a mixed-characteristic base.']
- **limit-rational-log-triviality** (hypothesis): Rational linear triviality of the log canonical class is part of the log Calabi–Yau hypothesis, rather than a conclusion for arbitrary projective pairs. Blocked use: ['Use the uniform index theorem to trivialize an adjoint class that is not rationally linearly trivial.']
- **limit-fixed-degree-principality** (hypothesis): The fixed-degree form and character arguments require an integral principal log canonical multiple. Rational linear triviality of that same multiple is insufficient. Blocked use: ['Supply only rational linear equivalence when invoking the fixed-degree character theorem.']
- **limit-substantial-companion-inputs** (formal_scope): The normal index and good-model theorems are substantial imported inputs. The descent and rank arguments do not independently replace their existence, nonvanishing, and semiampleness conclusions. Blocked use: ['Present the uniform index proof as independent of the normal index theorem or the good-model theorem.']
- **limit-hodge-gap-vanishing-and-rank** (hypothesis): The Hodge norm gap requires the specified Kähler geometry, semipositive stretching form with its pointwise rank restriction, and vanishing top cup product. Blocked use: ['Use the improved growth exponent for an arbitrary metric deformation or a class without the cup-product vanishing.']
- **limit-diagonal-local-positivity** (hypothesis): The diagonal estimate assumes canonical singularities, an integral principal canonical divisor, normalized ample rational polarization volume, and a strict Seshadri lower bound at very general smooth points. Blocked use: ['Invoke the diagonal rank estimate using only an ample divisor with no normalized volume or local positivity control.']
- **limit-rank-rational-images** (hypothesis): The dimension-only Hodge rank theorem requires the specified terminal factorial model, principal canonical divisor, vanishing resolution irregularity, and rationally connected resolutions of every smaller positive-dimensional rational image. Blocked use: ['Claim the rank theorem for every variety with trivial canonical class.']
- **limit-complete-systems-and-generic-data** (formal_scope): Section orders are measured using complete sufficiently divisible section spaces and very general or geometric generic data. Restricted ambient subsystems need not satisfy the same order assertions. Blocked use: ['Replace complete section spaces by a selected subsystem in the chain or vertical-order arguments.', 'Replace generic marks by arbitrary special points without a separate justification.']
- **limit-asymptotic-thresholds** (nonconstructive): Uniform final bounds do not imply uniform divisibility or asymptotic thresholds for all intermediate section calculations. Blocked use: ['Infer a uniform stopping degree for the intermediate section counts from the final dimension-dependent bound.']
- **limit-chain-tracking-fibre-inputs** (hypothesis): The moving-subvariety reduction requires marked covering families and the respective chain, separated-order-level, and small-volume Iitaka-fibre hypotheses. Its subsequence and generic-fibre conclusions do not apply to arbitrary selected subvarieties. Blocked use: ['Apply tracking without the required separated order levels.', 'Deduce vanishing adjoint volumes on arbitrary fibres from small polarization volume alone.']
- **limit-chain-field-attainment** (hypothesis): Attainment concerns chain fields from the specified low-order families with the stated strict parameter restriction. It does not identify every subfield with a contraction on a small terminal model. Blocked use: ['Apply the attainment lemma to an arbitrary rational fibration or an unrestricted subfield.']
- **limit-calibration-divisor-images** (hypothesis): Calibration requires the stated canonical model with vanishing resolution irregularity, bounded comparison-base degrees, and no prime divisor mapping into an omitted higher-codimension base subset. Blocked use: ['Extend base Weil-divisor pullbacks or apply the calibration functional without the divisor-image condition.']
- **limit-calibration-existential-selection** (nonconstructive): The calibrated divisor is obtained using separation, volume continuity, and rational approximation. This argument does not supply a finite numerical optimization routine. Blocked use: ['Advertise the calibration proof as an executable optimization algorithm with a certified stopping criterion.']
- **limit-vertical-jets-and-horizontal-counts** (formal_scope): The section-count contradiction requires both the vertical order bound and the horizontal slope argument. Injectivity of the vertical jet map controls the fibre span, rather than providing an inverse or a global section bound by itself. Blocked use: ['Treat the vertical jet map as invertible.', 'Infer the global section estimate from fibre rank without the horizontal slope and section estimates.']
- **limit-semistable-section-bound** (hypothesis): The linear dependence on sheaf rank is proved for torsion-free slope-semistable sheaves on complex projective space with its stated polarization. Applications to other sheaves require the additional reductions used in the proof. Blocked use: ['Apply the section estimate directly to arbitrary unstable sheaves or arbitrary polarized bases.']
- **limit-cover-stable-factor-reduction** (hypothesis): The character reduction uses the specified quasi-étale product decomposition and properties preserved under further connected normal quasi-étale covers. A decomposition lacking these cover-stable vanishing properties does not justify the factor-direction argument. - **limit-even-degree-residue-comparison** (hypothesis): Exact residue compatibility uses positive even degree. Odd degree retains the nodal sign and cannot be handled by silently imposing equality of branch residues. - **limit-minimal-stratum-restriction** (formal_scope): A crepant birational map need not restrict to a chosen minimal stratum. - **limit-graph-rescaling-is-conditional** (formal_scope): Spanning-tree rescaling is a finite conditional construction after the geometric argument annihilates all closed-walk products. It does not compute the uniform geometric exponent, and additional edges and loops remain compatibility conditions. Blocked use: ['Conclude global Cartierness solely from componentwise trivializations on the normalization.']

There is at most one abelian factor and at most one rationally connected factor $(F,H)$, where $(F,H)$ is klt, $H\\geq0$, and $K_F+H\\sim_{\\mathbb Q}0$; the entire pulled-back boundary is the pullback of $H$, and is zero if that factor is absent. They, and every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of them, have canonical singularities and Cartier trivial canonical class. Their reflexive form algebra is generated by a top form in the Calabi–Yau case and by a symplectic form in the symplectic case. In particular they have no reflexive one-forms or vector fields.*\n\n*The cover can be chosen so that every connected normal finite quasi-étale cover of $F$ is rationally connected. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove a uniform index theorem for connected projective semi-log-canonical log Calabi–Yau pairs in every fixed dimension at least four over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero. For boundary coefficients in a fixed finite rational set, a single multiple of the log canonical divisor is Cartier and linearly trivial. The multiple depends only on the dimension and coefficient set, not on the number of irreducible components. Together with the established theorem in dimensions at most three, this resolves the finite-rational-coefficient semi-log-canonical index conjecture.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The index of a log Calabi–Yau pair measures the degree in which its rationally trivial log canonical class becomes an actual trivial line bundle. A uniform index theorem asks whether that degree can be chosen from the dimension and the boundary coefficients alone. For a nonnormal pair, this question includes a descent problem: trivializations on the components of the normalization need not agree along the double locus.

We work over an algebraically closed field $k$ of characteristic zero. A scheme is *demi-normal* if it is reduced, satisfies Serre’s condition $S_2$, is seminormal, and has only nodes in codimension one. Let $X$ be a projective equidimensional demi-normal scheme, and let $B\ge0$ be a rational Weil divisor with no component in the nonnormal locus. Write $\nu:\bar X\to X$ for the normalization and $\bar C$ for its conductor divisor. The pair $(X,B)$ is *semi-log-canonical*, abbreviated slc, if $K_X+B$ is rational Cartier and $$(\bar X,\bar C+\nu_*^{-1}B)$$ is log canonical. These are the singularities that allow normal components to meet along a double locus while retaining adjunction and pluricanonical descent (Kollár 2013) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Kollar2013](#ref-Kollar2013)]. We call $(X,B)$ a log Calabi–Yau pair when, in addition, $K_X+B\sim_{\mathbb Q}0$: some positive Cartier multiple has trivial associated line bundle. The *index* of the pair is the least such positive integer.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $d\ge4$ and every finite set $\Phi\subset[0,1]\cap\mathbb Q$, there is an integer $a(d,\Phi)>0$ with the following property. Let $(X,B)$ be a connected projective equidimensional semi-log-canonical log Calabi–Yau pair of dimension $d$ over $k$, with every nonzero coefficient of $B$ in $\Phi$. Then $$a(d,\Phi)(K_X+B)\text{ is Cartier},
       \qquad \mathcal O_X\bigl(a(d,\Phi)(K_X+B)\bigr)\simeq\mathcal O_X.$$*

</div>

The bound is independent of the number of irreducible components of $X$ and of the number of log canonical strata on their birational models. It is a common trivializing multiple, so it clears the local Cartier indices and kills the remaining global torsion at the same time. Together with the theorem in dimensions at most three of Jiang and Liu (2021, Corollary 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)], this proves the finite-rational-coefficient slc index conjecture (Jiang and Liu 2021, Conjecture 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and relation to earlier work

The distinction between existence of an index and a uniform index is already visible in numerical-dimension-zero abundance. Gongyo proved that a numerically trivial log canonical divisor on a projective slc pair is rationally linearly trivial (Gongyo 2013, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Gongyo2013](#ref-Gongyo2013)]; the resulting trivializing multiple may depend on the pair. The index conjecture asks for a common multiple after fixing the dimension and the boundary coefficients. Its finite-rational-coefficient slc formulation is stated, for example, in Jiang and Liu (2021, Conjecture 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)]. The nonnormal setting is important because slc pairs occur as limits in moduli problems; see Kollár (2013) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Kollar2013](#ref-Kollar2013)] and the boundedness theory for polarized slc Calabi–Yau pairs in Birkar (2023) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Birkar2023](#ref-Birkar2023)].

Pluricanonical representations have long connected index problems to descent. Fujino developed admissible sections in his abundance theorem for slc threefolds (Fujino 2000) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Fujino2000](#ref-Fujino2000)], and formulated uniform boundedness of canonical representations for canonical varieties with trivial canonical divisor (Fujino 2001, Conjecture 3.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Fujino2001](#ref-Fujino2001)]. His local-index results and Ishii’s global-index theorem for non-klt log canonical threefold pairs with standard coefficients made this connection explicit (Fujino 2001; Ishii 2000) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Fujino2001](#ref-Fujino2001), [Ishii2000](#ref-Ishii2000)]. Here the standard coefficients are $1$ and $1-1/q$ for positive integers $q$. Fujino–Gongyo subsequently proved finiteness of the log pluricanonical representation for every projective lc pair with semiample log canonical divisor (Fujino and Gongyo 2014, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[FujinoGongyo2014](#ref-FujinoGongyo2014)]. Uniform boundedness of these finite images is the additional issue in the index problem.

Xu organized reductions among the normal index conjecture, its slc version, and boundedness of pluricanonical representations (Xu 2019) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Xu2019](#ref-Xu2019)]. In particular, his Theorem 1.11 gives the implication from normal indices in dimension $d$ and bounded representations in dimension $d-1$ to slc indices in dimension $d$. Jiang proved the global index bound for boundary-free klt threefolds (Jiang 2021, Corollary 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Jiang2021](#ref-Jiang2021)]. Jiang–Liu bounded the representations of lc surfaces with a fixed trivializing multiple, obtaining the slc index theorem in dimensions at most three and the non-klt lc case in dimension four (Jiang and Liu 2021, Theorem 1.4 and Corollaries 1.6–1.7) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)]. Xu also treated klt fourfold pairs with nonzero boundary (Xu 2019, Theorem 1.14) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Xu2019](#ref-Xu2019)].

Several more recent results give bounds under different geometric hypotheses. Birkar proved the index conjecture for rationally connected klt pairs in every dimension, allowing a fixed rational DCC coefficient set (Birkar 2025, Corollary 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Birkar2025](#ref-Birkar2025)]. Filipazzi–Mauri–Moraga obtained sharp bounds for coregularity-zero pairs, including slc pairs (Filipazzi, Mauri, and Moraga 2025) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[FMM2025](#ref-FMM2025)]; here coregularity zero means that a dlt model of the normalization with its induced boundary has a zero-dimensional minimal lc stratum. Figueroa–Filipazzi–Moraga–Peng developed corresponding index and complement results in low coregularity (Figueroa et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[FFMP2025](#ref-FFMP2025)]. Masamura proved boundedness of the indices of smooth Calabi–Yau fourfolds (Masamura 2025, Corollary 1.6) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Masamura2025](#ref-Masamura2025)]. Uniform bounds need not be numerically small: Singh constructed smooth Calabi–Yau varieties whose indices grow doubly exponentially with dimension (Singh 2026) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Singh2026](#ref-Singh2026)].

The proof here follows the established normal-to-slc strategy. It uses the uniform normal log canonical index theorem of the companion *Uniform Pluricanonical Iitaka Fibrations* (OpenAI 2026d, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[UPI](#ref-UPI)], together with the all-dimensional good-model theorem of *Log abundance in characteristic zero* (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 11.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[LA](#ref-LA)]. These are substantial inputs. Integral cohomology already bounds pluricanonical characters in terms of middle Betti numbers of suitable covers (Jiang and Liu 2021, Lemma 2.7) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)]. The additional uniformity comes from a rank bound for the rational Hodge structure generated by a holomorphic top form and the resulting character bound for arbitrary integral klt pairs with fixed index. The latter is the klt case of the bounded-representation problem formulated in Jiang and Liu (2021, Conjecture 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)]. The final comparison of conductor residues uses Kollár’s linking theorem and its even-degree residue compatibility (Kollár 2016, Theorem 10 and Proposition 14) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[KollarSources](#ref-KollarSources)].

The rank argument combines local positivity, moving subvarieties, and section counts. Its order propagation belongs to the Ein–Küchle–Lazarsfeld method (Ein, Küchle, and Lazarsfeld 1995) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[EKL1995](#ref-EKL1995)]; we use the precise chain and scalar constructions of OpenAI (2026b, secs. 5–6) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[U4](#ref-U4)] and OpenAI (2026d, sec. 6) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[UPI](#ref-UPI)]. Cotangent semipositivity (Greb, Kebekus, and Peternell 2016; Campana and Păun 2019) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[GKP2016](#ref-GKP2016), [CP2019](#ref-CP2019)], divisorial Zariski decomposition (Nakayama 2004; Boucksom 2004; Boucksom, Favre, and Jonsson 2009) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Nakayama2004](#ref-Nakayama2004), [Boucksom2004](#ref-Boucksom2004), [BFJ2009](#ref-BFJ2009)], and effective birationality (Birkar 2023) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Birkar2023](#ref-Birkar2023)] supply the positivity estimates. The character argument builds on Matsumura–Wang’s decomposition of klt log Calabi–Yau pairs (Matsumura and Wang 2025) <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[MatsumuraWang2025](#ref-MatsumuraWang2025)] and the singular Beauville–Bogomolov theory (Greb, Kebekus, and Peternell 2016; Druel 2018; Greb, Guenancia, and Kebekus 2019; Höring and Peternell 2019) <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[GKP2016](#ref-GKP2016), [Druel2018](#ref-Druel2018), [GGK2019](#ref-GGK2019), [HoringPeternell2019](#ref-HoringPeternell2019)]. For rationally connected factors we use Han–Jiang’s boundedness theorem and Jiang–Liu’s bounded-family representation theorem (Han and Jiang 2024; Jiang and Liu 2021) <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[HanJiang](#ref-HanJiang), [JiangLiu](#ref-JiangLiu)]. The proofs below isolate the new uniform estimates and explain the adaptations of these earlier constructions.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The character obstruction and the proof

We first describe the descent problem that dictates the proof. The uniform normal index theorem of OpenAI (2026d, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[UPI](#ref-UPI)] supplies one even integer $m$, depending only on $d$ and $\Phi$, for all the normalization components. On the $i$th component its trivialization can be written as a rational $m$-canonical form $\theta_i$ with the prescribed logarithmic poles. On a crepant divisorial log terminal model, logarithmic residue restricts this form to the conductor strata. At a generic node, the two residues differ by a nonzero scalar $r_e$; the existence of some global trivializing multiple ensures that the ratio is constant.

Make a finite graph whose vertices are normalization components and whose edges are the generic nodes. Rescaling $\theta_i$ changes its incident edge ratios. Thus simultaneous matching amounts to making every product of ratios around a closed walk equal to one. A uniform power of all the forms will accomplish this if the orders of these products are uniformly bounded.

Minimal log canonical strata supply the necessary interpretation of a closed walk. Their adjoint pairs are klt. Inside one normalization component, $\mathbb P^1$-linking identifies any two minimal strata by a crepant birational map that preserves the even-degree residue. Across an edge, we prove a corresponding birational residue comparison with multiplier $r_e$. A closed walk therefore induces a crepant birational self-map of one minimal klt stratum, whose action on its trivializing form is exactly the product of the edge ratios. Section <a href="#sec:residues" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:residues">7</a> carries out these comparisons, including nonsplit nodes and the final $S_2$ extension.

The main uniformity problem is consequently the following one. For a projective integral klt pair $(V,\Delta)$ satisfying $m(K_V+\Delta)\sim0$, bound, in terms of $\dim V$ and $m$, the order of the scalar by which a crepant birational self-map acts on a trivializing $m$-canonical form. We prove this in Theorem <a href="#thm:characters" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:characters">17</a>. The structural decomposition of such pairs separates a rationally connected factor, an abelian factor, and irreducible Calabi–Yau or symplectic factors. The rationally connected factor is controlled by boundedness and log pluricanonical representations. For the other factors, the character appears in integral middle cohomology.

The relevant cohomology is the smallest rational Hodge substructure $T(U)\subset H^n(U,\mathbb Q)$ containing the holomorphic top forms of a smooth projective resolution $U$. It is birationally invariant and carries a polarized integral lattice. A bound for its rank therefore bounds the degree of the cyclotomic polynomial of a top-form character. Theorem <a href="#thm:rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:rank">9</a> provides that rank bound under the rational-image hypotheses satisfied by the irreducible factors.

Two parts of the rank argument are useful independently. First, a dimension-dependent estimate for the variation of a Hodge norm gives a strict improvement over the largest possible metric growth whenever a specified top cup product vanishes. Applied to a class on the blowup of the diagonal, it bounds $\dim T(U)$ from a normalized volume and a lower Seshadri bound. These arguments occupy Sections <a href="#sec:hodge" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:hodge">3</a> and <a href="#sec:diagonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagonal">4</a>. Second, Section <a href="#sec:rank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rank">5</a> removes the Seshadri hypothesis. Small-order moving subvarieties determine rational fibrations; a maximal such fibration reduces the issue to vertical jets and horizontal section counts.
